<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7ED92958-A20C-4CB1-B94C-7667D1274879'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the perimeter of this square.
        
![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Area = $49 \ \text{cm}^{2}$

- $49 \ \text{cm}$
- $98 \ \text{cm}$
- $28 \ \text{cm}$
- $20 \ \text{cm}$
- $14 \ \text{cm}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square (in cm) $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a              &= 49 \\\\
a^2                     &= 49 \\\\
a                       &= \sqrt {49} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the square.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 4 \times a \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 28 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$28 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded

Let the side of the square (in cm) $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a              &= 49 \\\\
a^2                     &= 49 \\\\
a                       &= \sqrt {49} \\\\
a                       &= 7 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the square.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 4 \times a \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 7 \\\\
                        &= 28 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-6082C51C-48FB-4A15-B156-8BC33B276648'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate the area of the trapezium.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


- $50 \ \text{cm}^{2}$
- $44 \ \text{cm}^{2}$
- $40 \ \text{cm}^{2}$
- $42 \ \text{cm}^{2}$
- $48 \ \text{cm}^{2}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of trapezium} = \dfrac {\text{length} + \text{width}} {2} \times \text {height}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}                 &= \dfrac {8+12} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac {20} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 10} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac { \cancel 2 \times 10} {\cancel 2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= 10 \times 5\\\\
                            &= 50 \ \text{cm}^{2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$50 \ \text{cm}^{2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_trapezium_expanded

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}                 &= \dfrac {8+12} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac {20} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 10} {2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= \dfrac { \cancel 2 \times 10} {\cancel 2}\times 5\\\\
                            &= 10 \times 5\\\\
                            &= 50 \ \text{cm}^{2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50 \ \text{cm}^{2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9A5410BD-3EE5-409F-8688-DA50768205F0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A roll of wallpaper is $10 \ \text{m}$ long and $0.6 \ \text{m}$ wide. How many rolls of 
wallpaper are needed to cover a wall $3 \ \text{m}$ high and $8 \ \text{m}$ wide?

- $5$
- $4$
- $7$
- $10$
- $11$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of rolls}  &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the Wall}} {\text{Area of a wallpaper roll}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {10 \times 0.6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>B</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of rolls}  &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the Wall}} {\text{Area of a wallpaper roll}} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {10 \times 0.6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
                        &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>B</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F1B80E8B-8F3D-4D7C-B004-64E862ADAB50'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the perimeter of the compound shape.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


- $92 \ \text{cm}$
- $52 \ \text{cm}$
- $36 \ \text{cm}$
- $25 \ \text{cm}$
- $30 \ \text{cm}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

APTQ,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter is the total distance around the outside of a shape} \\\\
\text{The missing vertical side plus } \  4 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{must equal} \  10 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{The missing vertical side is} \  6 \  \text{cm}   \\\\
\text{The missing  horizontal side plus } \  3 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{must equal} \  8 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{The missing  horizontall side is} \  5 \  \text{cm}   \\\\
\text{We find the perimeter by adding the outside edges of our shape} \\\\
8  \ \text{cm} + 10 \ \text{cm} &= 18 \ \text{cm} \\\\
3  \ \text{cm} + 4 \ \text{cm} &= 7 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{We can add these four sides to make} \  25 \  \text{cm} \\\\
\text{We add the remaining two sides to} \  25 \  \text{cm} \\\\
25  \ \text{cm} + 6 \ \text{cm} &= 31 \ \text{cm} \\\\
31  \ \text{cm} + 5 \ \text{cm} &= 36 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{The perimeter of this compound shape is }  \ 36 \  \text{cm} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>APTQ,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter is the total distance around the outside of a shape} \\\\
\text{The missing vertical side plus } \  4 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{must equal} \  10 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{The missing vertical side is} \  6 \  \text{cm}   \\\\
\text{The missing  horizontal side plus } \  3 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{must equal} \  8 \  \text{cm} \ \ \text{The missing  horizontall side is} \  5 \  \text{cm}   \\\\
\text{We find the perimeter by adding the outside edges of our shape} \\\\
8  \ \text{cm} + 10 \ \text{cm} &= 18 \ \text{cm} \\\\
3  \ \text{cm} + 4 \ \text{cm} &= 7 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{We can add these four sides to make} \  25 \  \text{cm} \\\\
\text{We add the remaining two sides to} \  25 \  \text{cm} \\\\
25  \ \text{cm} + 6 \ \text{cm} &= 31 \ \text{cm} \\\\
31  \ \text{cm} + 5 \ \text{cm} &= 36 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text{The perimeter of this compound shape is }  \ 36 \  \text{cm} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>C</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A2279230-AADE-4793-B27A-4810B6805DE7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A square piece of card has perimeter $28 \ \text{cm}$. 
Ann cuts the card into two rectangles. 
The perimeter of one of the rectangles is $16 \ \text{cm}$. 
What is the  perimeter of the other rectangle?

- $26$
- $16$
- $14$
- $24$
- $20$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the side of the square $= a$

Let the width of the first rectangle $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a              &= 28 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {28} {4} \\\\ 
a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\ 
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 7} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                       &= 7  \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of first rectangle} &= 16 \\\\
2 \times (7 + b)                    &= 16 \\\\
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\ 
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 8} {2} \\\\ 
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 8} {\cancel 2} \\\\
7 + b                               &= 8 \\\\
b                                   &= 8 - 7 \\\\
b                                   &= 1 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of second rectangle} &=  2 \times (7 + (7-1)) \\\\
                                     &=  2 \times (7 + 6) \\\\
                                     &=  2 \times 13 \\\\
                                     &=  26 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$26 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

Let the side of the square $= a$

Let the width of the first rectangle $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a              &= 28 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {28} {4} \\\\ 
a                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {4} \\\\ 
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 7} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                       &= 7  \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of first rectangle} &= 16 \\\\
2 \times (7 + b)                    &= 16 \\\\
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {16} {2} \\\\ 
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 8} {2} \\\\ 
7 + b                               &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 8} {\cancel 2} \\\\
7 + b                               &= 8 \\\\
b                                   &= 8 - 7 \\\\
b                                   &= 1 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter of second rectangle} &=  2 \times (7 + (7-1)) \\\\
                                     &=  2 \times (7 + 6) \\\\
                                     &=  2 \times 13 \\\\
                                     &=  26 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$26 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-61A3F8DC-F527-4F5A-ACC5-9DD9A5F2E7FE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The area of the parallelogram is $38 \ \text{cm}^2$. Find the length of its base.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


- $9.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $8.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $7.5 \ \text{cm}$
- $7.25 \ \text{cm}$
- $9.25 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of parallelogram} = \text{base} \times \text{height}$


Let the base of the parallelogram $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times b                    &= 38 \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {38} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {4 \times 9.5} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 9.5} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
b                             &= 9.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the base of the parallelogram $= 9.5 \ \text{cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>A</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$9.5 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_parallelogram_expanded

Let the base of the parallelogram $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times b                    &= 38 \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {38} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {4 \times 9.5} {4} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 9.5} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
b                             &= 9.5 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the base of the parallelogram $= 9.5 \ \text{cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>A</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9.5 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Four rectangles each of length $14 \ \text{cm}$ and width $6 \ \text{cm}$ are arranged 
to form the square shape shown below (the diagram is not drawn to scale).

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Find:

The perimeter of this outer square shape

- $90 \ \text{cm}$
- $27 \ \text{cm}$
- $80 \ \text{cm}$
- $20 \ \text{cm}$
- $40 \ \text{cm}$  

</div>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Side of the square}  &= 14 + 6 \\\\
                            &= 20 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text {Perimeter}           &= 4 \times 20 \\\\
                            &= 80 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='option'>
<p>C</p>
</div>
<div class='answer'>

$80 \ \text{cm}$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0015986A-8689-4697-907E-D8FF9E05B338'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Here are three squares that overlap.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The non-overlapping parts of the squares have a total area of $90 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

The areas of overlapping parts are $2 \ \text{cm}^{2}$, 
$3 \ \text{cm}^{2}$ and $4 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

Work out the area of one of the squares. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

According to question,
 
area of non-overlapping squares $=90 cm^2$

area of non-overlapping of one squares $=90 cm^2 \div 3$

$ 90 \div{3} = 30cm^2$

area of overlapping parts $=2cm^2,3cm^2,4cm^2$

area of one square $= area of non-overlapping of one square + area of 2 overlapping parts$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30cm^2 + 2cm^2 +3cm^2 \\\\
&=   35 cm^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$35 cm^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>According to question,
 
area of non-overlapping squares $=90 cm^2$

area of non-overlapping of one squares $=90 cm^2 \div 3$

$ 90 \div{3} = 30cm^2$

area of overlapping parts $=2cm^2,3cm^2,4cm^2$

area of one square $= area of non-overlapping of one square + area of 2 overlapping parts$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 30cm^2 + 2cm^2 +3cm^2 \\\\
&=   35 cm^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$35 cm^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-19234F0C-4C85-4F00-8A0C-D18A9430B6A0'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Four strips of paper are stuck on a table as shown. Each one is a rectangle that is $12 \ \text{cm}$ long and $2 \ \text{cm}$ wide.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What area of the table is covered? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Since the rectangles are overlapping at four places, we need to subtract those area from the total.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the table covered} &= \text {Total area of rectangles} - \text {overlapping area} \\\\
                                  &= (12 \times 2 \times 4) - (2 \times 2 \times 4) \\\\
                                  &= 96 - 16 \\\\
                                  &= 80 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$80 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

Since the rectangles are overlapping at four places, we need to subtract those area from the total.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of the table covered} &= \text {Total area of rectangles} - \text {overlapping area} \\\\
                                  &= (12 \times 2 \times 4) - (2 \times 2 \times 4) \\\\
                                  &= 96 - 16 \\\\
                                  &= 80 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$80 \ \text {cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-80391C1A-13B4-4C02-A048-8C0B0C4AF7B9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A cuboid has faces with areas of $24 \ \text{cm}^{2}, 32 \ \text{cm}^{2}$ and $48 \ \text{cm}^{2}$.

What are the lengths of its edges? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of first edge of cuboid $= l$

Let the length of second edge of cuboid $= w$

Let the length of third edge cuboid $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times w                                      &= 24 \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {l} \\\\
w \times h                                      &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24} {l} \times h                        &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24h} {l}                                &= 32 \\\\
24h                                             &= 32 \times l \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {32 \times l} {24} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
l \times \dfrac{32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{l \times 32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{32 \times l^2} {24}                      &= 48 \\\\
32 \times l^2                                   &= 48 \times 24 \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {48 \times 24} {32} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {16 \times 3 \times 2 \times 12} {16 \times 2} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times 12} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
l^2                                             &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
l^2                                             &= 36 \\\\
l                                               &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
l                                               &= 6 \ \text {cm} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
w                                               &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
6 \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {48} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 8} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                               &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the edges of the cuboid are $6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the length of first edge of cuboid $= l$

Let the length of second edge of cuboid $= w$

Let the length of third edge cuboid $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times w                                      &= 24 \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {l} \\\\
w \times h                                      &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24} {l} \times h                        &= 32 \\\\
\dfrac {24h} {l}                                &= 32 \\\\
24h                                             &= 32 \times l \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {32 \times l} {24} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
l \times \dfrac{32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{l \times 32 \times l} {24}               &= 48 \\\\
\dfrac{32 \times l^2} {24}                      &= 48 \\\\
32 \times l^2                                   &= 48 \times 24 \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {48 \times 24} {32} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {16 \times 3 \times 2 \times 12} {16 \times 2} \\\\
l^2                                             &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times 12} {\cancel {16} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
l^2                                             &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
l^2                                             &= 36 \\\\
l                                               &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
l                                               &= 6 \ \text {cm} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
w                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
w                                               &= 4 \ \text {cm} \\\\
l \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
6 \times h                                      &= 48 \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {48} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {6 \times 8} {6} \\\\
h                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 8} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
h                                               &= 8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the edges of the cuboid are $6 \ \text {cm}, \ 4 \ \text {cm}, \ 8 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many strips do I need in total?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.
The lawn measurement is given in $\text{metres}$ while the strip measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the lawn measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total strips required}           &= \dfrac {\text {Width of lawn}} {\text {Width of strips}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \ \text {meters}} {50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100} {50} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                        &= 20 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
The lawn measurement is given in $\text{metres}$ while the strip measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the lawn measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total strips required}           &= \dfrac {\text {Width of lawn}} {\text {Width of strips}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \ \text {meters}} {50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100 \ \text {cm}} {50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 100} {50} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 50 \times 2} {50} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {50} \times 2} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                        &= 20 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance walked}                 &= \text {Number of strips} \times 30 \\\\
                                        &= 40 \times 30 \\\\
                                        &= 1200 \ \text {metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>

<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I can push my lawnmower at a constant speed of $30 \ \text{metres}$ per minute and it takes me an extra $15 \ \text{seconds}$ 
to turn the mower round at the end of every strip. The mower itself takes $15 \ \text{seconds}$ 
to turn on. How long does it take me to mow my lawn?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.
The lawnmower speed is given in $\text{metres per minute}$ while the mower turn speed is given in $\text{seconds}$.

Let's convert the lawnmower speed into $\text{meters per seconds}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken to mow the lawn}          &= \text {(Number of strips)} \times 1 \ \text {min} + \text {(Number of strips - 1)} \times 15 \ \text {sec} + 15 \ \text {sec} \\\\
                                            &= 40 \times 1 \times 60 \ \text {sec} + 39 \times  15 \ \text {sec} + 15 \ \text {sec} \\\\
                                            &= 40 \times 1 \times 60  + 39 \times  15 + 15 \\\\
                                            &= 2400 + 585 + 15 \\\\
                                            &= 3000 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3000} {60} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \times 50} {60} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times 50} {\cancel {60}} \\\\
                                            &= 50 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50 \ \text {minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
The lawnmower speed is given in $\text{metres per minute}$ while the mower turn speed is given in $\text{seconds}$.

Let's convert the lawnmower speed into $\text{meters per seconds}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time taken to mow the lawn}          &= \text {(Number of strips)} \times 1 \ \text {min} + \text {(Number of strips - 1)} \times 15 \ \text {sec} + 15 \ \text {sec} \\\\
                                            &= 40 \times 1 \times 60 \ \text {sec} + 39 \times  15 \ \text {sec} + 15 \ \text {sec} \\\\
                                            &= 40 \times 1 \times 60  + 39 \times  15 + 15 \\\\
                                            &= 2400 + 585 + 15 \\\\
                                            &= 3000 \ \text {seconds} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {3000} {60} \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {60 \times 50} {60} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {60} \times 50} {\cancel {60}} \\\\
                                            &= 50 \ \text {minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$50 \ \text {minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the area of Whasham.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area of Whasham}      &= 20 \times 8 \\\\
                             &= 160 \ \text {km}^2
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

$
\begin {aligned}

                             &= 160 \ \text {km}^2
\end {aligned}
$

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The area of land which consists of forest but no mountain is $32 \ \text{km}^2$ and the area which is both forest and 
mountain is $6 \ \text{km}^2$. The area of water is $67 \ \text{km2}$. 

Work out the area of land which consists of mountain but no forest.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area}                 &= \text {Land} - \text {Water} - \text {Forest} \\\\
                             &= 160 - 67 - (32 + 6) \\\\
                             &= 160 - 67 - 38 \\\\
                             &= 55 \ \text {km} ^ 2
\end {aligned}
$

Area of land which consists of mountain but no forest $= 55 \ \text {km}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$55 \ \text {km} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Area}                 &= \text {Land} - \text {Water} - \text {Forest} \\\\
                             &= 160 - 67 - (32 + 6) \\\\
                             &= 160 - 67 - 38 \\\\
                             &= 55 \ \text {km} ^ 2
\end {aligned}
$

Area of land which consists of mountain but no forest $= 55 \ \text {km}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$55 \ \text {km} ^ 2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A total of $121$ people live on the island. If $81$ people live on land which is mountainous and $48$ people live on land which is forested, how many live in the region which is both mountainous and forested?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of people}     &= \text {People in mountainous land} + \text {People in forested land} - \text {Total people} \\\\
                             &= 81 + 48 - 121 \\\\
                             &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

$8$ people live in the region which is both mountainous and forested.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of people}     &= \text {People in mountainous land} + \text {People in forested land} - \text {Total people} \\\\
                             &= 81 + 48 - 121 \\\\
                             &= 8
\end {aligned}
$

$8$ people live in the region which is both mountainous and forested.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C3229D2F-CF27-4E5A-A8CC-334AD15D2A96'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find the area of a square with a perimeter of $36 \ \text{cm}$.

Find the perimeter of a square with an area of $121 \ \text{cm}^2$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a           &= 36\\\\
a                    &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                    &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{4}} \\\\
a                    &= 9 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate the area of square.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area}    &= 9 \times 9 \\\\
                &= 81 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a             &= 121 \\\\
a^2                    &= 121 \\\\
a                      &= \sqrt {121} \\\\
a                      &= 11 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's calculate the perimeter of square.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}      &= 4 \times 11 \\\\
                       &= 44 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$81 \ \text {cm}^2 $

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$44 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded

$

Let's calculate the area of square.

                &= 81 \ \text {cm}^2

$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F1F1A10E-00DD-4BD6-8869-3317F652EBF6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Stuart's patio is $5 \ \text{metres}$ long and $5 \ \text{metres}$ wide.

Stuart wants to cover the patio with paving stones. Each paving stone is $50 \ \text{centimetres}$ long and 
$50 \ \text{centimetres}$ wide.

How many paving stones does Stuart need to buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
.
The patio measurement is given in $\text{meters}$ while the stone measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the patio measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the patio}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \ \text{m} \times 5 \ \text{m}} {50 \ \text{cm} \times 50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \times 5 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} {50 \ \text{cm} \times 50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 100 \times 5 \times 100} {50  \times 50} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 \times 10 \times 5 \times 10 \times 10} {5 \times 10 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times 10 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                         &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                         &= 100
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note.
The patio measurement is given in $\text{meters}$ while the stone measurement is given in $\text{cm}$.

Let's convert the patio measurements into $\text{cm}$.

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Number of stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the patio}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \ \text{m} \times 5 \ \text{m}} {50 \ \text{cm} \times 50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 100 \ \text{cm} \times 5 \times 100 \ \text{cm}} {50 \ \text{cm} \times 50 \ \text{cm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 100 \times 5 \times 100} {50  \times 50} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {5 \times 10 \times 10 \times 5 \times 10 \times 10} {5 \times 10 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times 10 \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel {5} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                         &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                         &= 100
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$100$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Stuart has $\pounds 200$ left after buying the paving stones. Stuart wants to put a picket fence around **three sides** of his patio.

Each panel of picket fencing is $1 \ \text{metre}$ long and costs $\pounds 10.50$. 
Stuart will also have to pay a delivery charge of $\pounds 5$ regardless of the number of panels of picket fencing he purchases.

How much money does Stuart have left after buying the picket fencing?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of panels}            &= 3 \times \dfrac {\text {Length of a side}} {\text {Length of a panel}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times \dfrac {5} {1} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
                                    &= 15 \\\\
\text {Cost of panels}              &= 10.50 \times 15 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 157.5 \\\\
\text {Money left}                  &= \text {Total Money} - \text {Cost of panels} - \text {Delivery charge} \\\\
                                    &= 200 - 157.5 - 5 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 37.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 37.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of panels}            &= 3 \times \dfrac {\text {Length of a side}} {\text {Length of a panel}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times \dfrac {5} {1} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 5 \\\\
                                    &= 15 \\\\
\text {Cost of panels}              &= 10.50 \times 15 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 157.5 \\\\
\text {Money left}                  &= \text {Total Money} - \text {Cost of panels} - \text {Delivery charge} \\\\
                                    &= 200 - 157.5 - 5 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 37.5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 37.5$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

What is the area of this triangle?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


As per the diagram,

$
\beign{aligned}
\text {Area}            &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 5 \times 4 \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 10 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text {cm}^2$

                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                        &= 5 \times 2 \\\\
                        &= 10 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

By thinking of two different ways to work out the area of the triangle, calculate the length of arrow.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


Let the length of the arrow $= h$

As, per the diagram,

$
\beign{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times 10 \times h        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 8 \times 6 \\\\
\dfrac {10 \times h} {2}                 &= \dfrac {8 \times 6} {2}  \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 6 \times 2} {2 \times 10} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2} {2 \times 10} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2} {2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {2} \times 5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {24} {5} \\\\
h                                        &= 4.8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the arrow $= 4.8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4.8 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_triangle_expanded

Let the length of the arrow $= h$

As, per the diagram,

$
\beign{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times 10 \times h        &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 8 \times 6 \\\\
\dfrac {10 \times h} {2}                 &= \dfrac {8 \times 6} {2}  \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 6 \times 2} {2 \times 10} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2} {2 \times 10} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times 2 \times 2} {2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3 \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {2} \times 5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {5} \\\\
h                                        &= \dfrac {24} {5} \\\\
h                                        &= 4.8 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the arrow $= 4.8 \ \text {cm}$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

What length of fence does he need to make this pen?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}                &= 2 \times (8 + 2) \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
                                 &= 20 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Length of fence}          &= \text {Perimeter} - 8 \\\\
                                 &= 20 - 8 \\\\
                                 &= 12 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

$
\begin{aligned}

                                 &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
                                 &= 20 \ \text {m} \\\\
\text {Length of fence}          &= \text {Perimeter} - 8 \\\\
                                 &= 20 - 8 \\\\
                                 &= 12 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

</code></pre>

He decides to buy $15 \ \text{metres}$ of fence. 

Here is his new plan

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Find the length of his new pen.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of new pen $= l$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 + l + 3               &= 15 \\\\
l + 6                   &= 15 \\\\
l                       &= 15 - 6 \\\\
l                       &= 9 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

l                       &= 15 - 6 \\\\
l                       &= 9 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Farmer Fred decides that he would like to build a square pen with his $15 \ \text{metres}$ of fence as shown below:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


How long should each side be?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of side $= a$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + a               &= 15 \\\\
3a                      &= 15 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {15} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                       &= 5 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Each side should be $5 \ \text {cm}$ long.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \ \text {cm}$

</div>

a                       &= \dfrac {15} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 5} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                       &= 5 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Each side should be $5 \ \text {cm}$ long.
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

Given that $1 \ \text{metre}$ of fence costs $\pounds 12$, 
find how much it will cost Farmer Fred to buy $15 \ \text{metres}$ of fence.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total cost}          &= \text {Cost of 1 \ \text {metre} fence } \times \text {Total fence} \\\\
                            &= 12 \times 15 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 180
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 180$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$

                            &= 12 \times 15 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 180
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table below for each rectangle.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the width of the rectangle A $= w$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 \times w              &= 24 \\\\
6w                      &= 24 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {24} {6} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {6 \times 4} {6} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {6} \times 4} {\cancel {6}} \\\\
w                       &= 4 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}               &= 2 \times (6 + 4) \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
                                &= 20 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the height of the rectangle B $= h$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
h \times 3              &= 24 \\\\
3h                      &= 24 \\\\
h                       &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
h                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
h                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 8} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
h                       &= 8 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}               &= 2 \times (8 + 3) \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                &= 22 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the width of the rectangle A $= w$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times w              &= 24 \\\\
2w                      &= 24 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {24} {2} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 12} {6} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 12} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
w                       &= 12 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}               &= 2 \times (12 + 2) \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 14 \\\\
                                &= 28 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$4 \ \text {m}, \ 20 \ \text {m}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the height and width of a rectangle with area $24 \ \text{m}^2$ which has a larger perimeter 
than the $3$ rectangles above.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


Let the width of the rectangle $= w$

Let the height of the rectangle $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times h          &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

We need to find the factor pairs of 24 with maximum sum to find a rectangle with area $24 \ \text {m}^2$ which has larger perimeter.

Factor Pairs of $24$

$
\begin{aligned}
1 \times 24 \\\\
2 \times 12 \\\\
3 \times 8 \\\\
4 \times 6 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$1 + 24$ has the maximum value.

$
\begin{aligned}
h  &= 1 \ \text {m} \\\\
w  &= 24 \ \text {m} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Height $= 1 \ \text {m}$

Width $= 24 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \ \text {m}, \ 24 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

$

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-CC765B1A-2C48-4EA1-BE5D-B7BC07413F8B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Farmer Jack and Farmer Giles each have a rectangular field.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Farmer Jack’s field has an area of $24 \ \text{m}^2$.

Its length is $8 \ \text{m}$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the width of Farmer Jack’s field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


Let the width of the field $= w$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \times w                 &= 24 \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {24} {8} \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {8} \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel{8}} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
w                          &= 3 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Width of the rectangle field $= 3 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text{m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

Let the width of the field $= w$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
8 \times w                 &= 24 \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {24} {8} \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {8} \\\\
w                          &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel{8}} {\cancel{8}} \\\\
w                          &= 3 \ \text{m}
\end{aligned}
$

Width of the rectangle field $= 3 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \ \text{m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out the perimeter of Farmer Jack’s field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}                &= 2 \times (8 + 3) \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                 &= 22 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$22 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}                &= 2 \times (8 + 3) \\\\
                                 &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                 &= 22 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Farmer Giles’ field has a perimeter of $20 \ \text{m}$.

The width of Farmer Giles’ field is $4 \ \text{m}$. 

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Work out the length of Farmer Giles’ field.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the length $= l$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times (4 + l)                 &= 20 \\\\
8 + 2l                           &= 20 \\\\
2l                               &= 20 - 8 \\\\
2l                               &= 12 \\\\
l                                &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
l                                &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
l                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
l                                &= 6 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

l                                &= 6 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6 \ \text {m}$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8B7C8089-6C3E-4010-9B82-DDD78E28A7D4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A farmer wants to put a fence along one edge of his field,  
which is $480 \ \text{m}$ long. Every $4 \ \text{m}$, a post is needed to hold the rails up. 

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


How many posts does he need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total posts}         &= \dfrac {\text {Length of fence}} {\text {Length for a post}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {480} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 120} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 120} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                            &= 120
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total posts}         &= \dfrac {\text {Length of fence}} {\text {Length for a post}} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {480} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {4 \times 120} {4} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 120} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                            &= 120
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$120$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8CFFC8B9-38B4-4B21-8408-CE0D03137FD7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The area of this rectangle is $24 \ \text{cm}^2$. What is its perimeter in cm?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let the length of the rectangle $= l$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times 4          &= 24 \\\\
4l                  &= 24 \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {24} {4} \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {4} \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 6} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
l                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 2 \times (6 + 4) \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
                        &= 20 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle

Let the length of the rectangle $= l$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
l \times 4          &= 24 \\\\
4l                  &= 24 \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {24} {4} \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {4 \times 6} {4} \\\\
l                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 6} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
l                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 2 \times (6 + 4) \\\\
                        &= 2 \times 10 \\\\
                        &= 20 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E7D1BED3-00FC-4923-94CA-88CE3E83BB8C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

This shape is made from $9$ identical squares.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The area of the shape is $144 \ \text{cm}^2$

What is the perimeter of the shape?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square $= s$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 \times s ^ 2  &= 144 \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {16 \times 9} {9} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= 16 \\\\
s               &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
s               &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

*TODO: Add a description on counting all the sides of the shape which makes the perimeter*.

Let's find the perimeter of the shape.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter} &= 20 \times 4 \\\\
                 &= 80 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$80 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded

rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square

Let the side of the square $= s$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 \times s ^ 2  &= 144 \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {144} {9} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {16 \times 9} {9} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= \dfrac {16 \times \cancel {9}} {\cancel {9}} \\\\
s ^ 2           &= 16 \\\\
s               &= \sqrt {16} \\\\
s               &= 4 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

*TODO: Add a description on counting all the sides of the shape which makes the perimeter*.

Let's find the perimeter of the shape.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Perimeter} &= 20 \times 4 \\\\
                 &= 80 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$80 \ \text{cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C56E63F7-3A43-43A3-BC1A-1FC3E09AE47C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

What is the area of this shape?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Show the diagram divided into rectangle and triangle with the calculations for the parts of the shape which do not have measurements shown.*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


As per the diagram, the shape can be divided into a triangle and a rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area}     &= \text{Area of the triangle} + \text{Area of the rectangle} \\\\
                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 4 + 2 \times 4 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 4} {2} + 8 \\\\
                &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 2 \times 4} {\cancel {2}} + 8 \\\\
                &= 2 \times 4 + 8 \\\\
                &= 8 + 8 \\\\
                &= 16 \ \text{units}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \ \text{units}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What are the values of $x$ and $y$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 + x + 2           &= 8 \\\\
x + 6               &= 8 \\\\
x                   &= 8 - 6 \\\\
x                   &= 2 \ \text {cm} \\\\
y + (6 - 2 - 2) + y &= 14 \\\\
2y + 2              &= 14 \\\\
2y                  &= 14 - 2 \\\\
2y                  &= 12 \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$x = 2 \ \text {cm} \ , y = 6 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

y                   &= \dfrac {12} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {2 \times 6} {2} \\\\
y                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 6} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
y                   &= 6 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The perimeter of the figure is $160\ \text{cm}$.

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the length of the side of each small square.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the length of the side $= a$

Number of sides included in the shape $= 16$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \times a             &= 160 \\\\
16a                     &= 160 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {160} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {16 \times 10} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 10} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                       &= 10 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the side of each small square $= 10 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the length of the side $= a$

Number of sides included in the shape $= 16$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \times a             &= 160 \\\\
16a                     &= 160 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {160} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {16 \times 10} {16} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {16} \times 10} {\cancel {16}} \\\\
a                       &= 10 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Length of the side of each small square $= 10 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of a square}            &= 10 \times 10 \\\\
                                    &= 100 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>

Find the area of the whole figure.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area}                        &= \text {Number of squares} \times \text {Area of a square} \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times (10 \times 10) \\\\
                                    &= 9 \times 100 \\\\
                                    &= 900 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$900 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
